#2972. Packers Winners and Losers: Week 12 vs. the Rams

On Thanksgiving Eve, the Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams faced off in a game which had heavy impact on the playoff chances for both teams. RG Kevin Zeitler made his first start for the Packers. EDGE Micah Parsons made his long awaited return after sustaining a torn ACL during the 2025 season. It was also another chapter in the Matt LaFleur vs. Sean McVay saga, and, for the first time since the two both became NFL head coaches, McVay and the Rams came out victorious, 26-13. Let's take a look at what worked, and what didn't.

It feels so good typing these words right now. After wondering what the heck was taking so long with the return of the Packers star edge rusher after a December 2025 ACL tear, it was revealed that the damage was much more severe than many of us realized. Last night, finally healthy, Parsons made his first start of the 2026 season, playing about 37% of total defensive snaps. And he looked…great!

Parsons nearly got to QB Matthew Stafford a few times, and then just before halftime, we all got to see the sack we'd been waiting 346 days for. With how things have been called all year for the Packers, I was certain this would be flagged for roughing the passer. Parsons finished the night with 1 sack and 2 QB hits, and was credited with 4 pressures on 26 snaps. Good stuff, and it'll only get better.

WR Christian Watson was the only reliable pass catcher for the Packers last night, and he was knocked out of the game in the first half after taking a nasty blow to the helmet, which saw Rams S Kam Kinchens ejected. Prior to the hit, Watson was the only one able to hold onto the ball or…do anything right, really. The routes were nice. The hands were strong. He finished the night catching all 4 targets for 65 yards and 1 TD. His concussion status will be one to monitor as the Packers head into their next game next Sunday.

The Packers have had a problem with TE Tucker Kraft all season, and now, it's got me begging them to stop giving him the ball. Kraft has had some pretty egregious drops this year, has played like he's scared of getting hit, and has just been generally BAD. Last night, he logged his second fumble of the year, and the Rams were able to turn it into 3 more points. Kraft finished the night catching 3 of 5 targets for 24 yards. They don't really have any other options at TE, so I guess we just hope for the best? And cry a little?

Last night was the first time I've seen QB Jordan Love visibly angry, and it came on the heels of an interception that seemed to have been caused by WR Matthew Golden running the wrong route. Golden has been great this year. He even showed flashes of that last night with his big 39 yard gain, but this error killed any sort of momentum the Packers were developing. Golden finished the night with 1 catch on 5 targets for 39 yards.

It may not be a good idea to stop running and then just watch as the defender intercepts your quarterback.

We can blame the quarterback (not really), we can blame the offensive line, we can blame the receivers, we can blame the run defense, we can blame the head coach! At the end of the day, this goes back to GM Brian Gutekunst, who put the team together. Now—there are things he can't control, like the drops, and the injury bug flying through the locker room. But the poor depth at offensive line has been the catalyst for almost all of the bad things to happen to the Packers offense this season.

Jordan Love has been running for his life. He's being hit while throwing. The RB room doesn't stand a chance. The receivers have had almost no time to get open. An even mediocre offensive line fixes a lot of this. By failing to put together an o-line group that can sustain any sort of run game, any sort of passing game other than "sling it and hope for the best", Brian Gutekunst has bungled the 2026 roster.

The 6-5 Packers make their next appearance next Sunday, December 6, against the 3-7 New Orleans Saints. The game is at 1 pm ET/12 pm CT, and yes, that's a threat.

#2971. Coca-Cola Is the Most Boring Stock I Own. After 64 Years of Dividend Raises, I'll Never Sell.

It's a blue chip dividend stock that you don't have to second-guess.

By Stefon Walters – Nov 26, 2026 at 7:39AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Coca-Cola ›

I consider myself adventurous, but I often prefer boring when it comes to the stocks I own. I have my fair share of growth stocks, for sure, but my staple "boring" stock is Coca-Cola (KO -0.03%).

Although Coca-Cola is up over 40% this year (as of Nov. 24), its main appeal has long been its dividend. It has increased its annual dividend for 64 consecutive years, making it one of the market's premier Dividend Kings ( companies with at least 50 consecutive years of dividend hikes). Considering that and its sustained success, it's a stock I'll never sell.

You can count on one hand the number of brands more well-known globally than Coca-Cola. Its products (especially its namesake soda) are available in virtually every part of the world, and its asset-light business model enables it to operate efficiently.

Usually, if a company wants distribution somewhere, it needs plants, warehouses, and delivery fleets in that market. In Coca-Cola's case, it relies on bottling and distribution partners to do so. Instead of owning factories and delivery fleets around the world, Coca-Cola sells concentrates and syrups to its partners and lets them do the heavy lifting.

Keeping its business asset-light lets Coca-Cola maintain high margins and an ultra-safe dividend. You don't get to 64 years of dividend increases without consistent, high-margin cash flows.

Not many companies fit the "recession-proof" narrative, but Coca-Cola is one of them. Even when the economy slows and money is tight, people still buy their favorite Coca-Cola brands. That's the beauty of its brand moat.

And despite being the undisputed leader in its space, Coca-Cola hasn't gotten complacent with its drink portfolio. It constantly refines its lineup to adjust to changing consumer preferences. Just six years ago, it had over 400 brands in its portfolio. Today, it has around half of that.

When you have an option for virtually every occasion, you keep customers in your ecosystem. It's how the company has sustained success for so long.

Coca-Cola is a premium business that's priced like one. At the time of this writing, it's trading at 28.9 times its earnings, which is higher than its average over the past few years and higher than the S&P 500's (^GSPC -0.25%)23.7 P/E ratio. The stock is still a buy, but after a 40% run-up this year, the upside may be more limited. Luckily, that doesn't affect its dividend.

Coca-Cola is always a good long-term investment, but with bear-market fears lingering, a short-term silver lining is that the stock typically outperforms the market during sell-offs. Past results don't guarantee future performance, but Coca-Cola has strong momentum at a time when the tech sector is dragging down the broader market.

#2968. Is Anthropic Joining the S&P 500? Here's What Investors Need to Know.

Don't expect major changes to your ETF portfolio.

By David Dierking – Nov 26, 2026 at 8:03AM EST

10 stocks we like better than S&P 500 Index ›

Anthropic (ANT +1.08%) just completed the largest IPO in history, raising around $93.5 billion at $83 per share. The stock closed Nov. 20 at $88.91.

Given that it's now one of the largest publicly traded companies in the world, you might assume its addition to the S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.25%) is imminent. It isn't, actually, and won't be for a while. But the stock will show up in several other major indexes within days.

When Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +1.30%) debuted earlier this year, there was a lot of discussion around how the major indexes would handle it. Typically, companies go public at much smaller market caps, not with market caps over $1 trillion. Index committees needed to decide whether to "fast track" these mega IPOs into the indexes sooner, given their outsize influence.

Several providers made changes. But Standard & Poor's did not. It elected to keep the existing 12-month waiting period and generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP) income requirements -- companies need to be profitable in the most recent quarter as well as the sum of the past four quarters in order to qualify.

Anthropic, so far, falls far short on the GAAP income requirement, reporting a net loss of roughly $104 billion in the first nine months of this year.

Given these requirements, the earliest we're likely to see Anthropic in the S&P 500 is late 2027.

The S&P 500 will actually be the exception, not the rule, when it comes to adding Anthropic.

The Nasdaq-100 index updated its inclusion criteria as a result of the SpaceX IPO. Now, if a company's entire market capitalization ranks within the top 40 current constituents in the index, it can be added to the index after just 15 trading days. Since Anthropic is listed on the Nasdaq, it could show up in the Invesco QQQ ETF (QQQ -0.65%) before the end of the year.

The Morningstar indexes, which acquired the widely-used CRSP indexes, could add Anthropic after the close of the fifth trading day. The Vanguard Total Stock Market ETF (VTI -0.18%) would be one of the biggest funds impacted by this. FTSE Russell works on a similar timeline.

A lot of index funds will weight stocks according to the number of publicly available shares, not necessarily the total market cap. Since only about 5% of Anthropic's total market cap is now traded publicly, its starting weight will likely be small. Vanguard has projected only a 0.055% to 0.15% weighting for Anthropic in the Vanguard Total Stock Market ETF.

When and if additional shares become publicly available, the anticipated weighting of these stocks would likely grow along with it.

If you really want to own Anthropic, you should probably just buy Anthropic.

The stock won't show up at all in the S&P 500 for the foreseeable future. For many of the major large-cap indexes and corresponding ETFs, the initial weight probably won't get much above 0.25%. The exception might be the Nasdaq-100, the Invesco QQQ ETF, and the Invesco Nasdaq 100 ETF (QQQM -0.66%). They could see an allocation closer to 1% given the more concentrated nature of that index.

It'll likely be a similar story to what we saw when SpaceX went public: a lot of hype but minimal impact to the ETF marketplace and your portfolio in general.

Long-term investors almost certainly shouldn't be making big asset allocation changes based on this. Even if you want to overweight tech based on a belief that Anthropic might spark the sector, your tech ETFs aren't going to meaningfully change in the future.

#2965. Knicks 121, Cavs 102: Scenes from a second-half bench bash

The Knicks' hopes of repeating as NBA Cup champions hinged on beating Cleveland (9-10) tonight and Miami on Friday, while Philadelphia had to beat Miami (which they did) and Indiana has to topple Philadelphia. Even then, advancement would come down to point differential, meaning New York (12-6) would likely need to win both games by double digits.

Consider the mission half completed. As you could deduce from the blazing blue court at Madison Square Garden, tonight's tilt was a Cup game. Three Knicks were sidelined by injury: OG Anunoby, Miles McBride, and Jordan Clarkson. Anunoby's absence was felt as the Cavaliers scored 61 first-half points (and a lopsided free-throw disparity stung, too). The adjustments Mike Brown made in the locker room paid dividends on the court. His squad applied stingier defense, got their turnovers under control, won the fourth quarter 39-17, and flipped the game to win, 121-102.

Of the six Knicks with double-digit points tonight, Jose Alvarado's barrage of fourth-quarter triples and his season-high / team-high 21 were arguably the most impactful. On a short-handed night, and with multiple starters playing through injury, Jose was once again prepared to step into the spotlight and contribute meaningful minutes. What a professional! What depth!

Three consecutive turnovers didn't help (two by Karl-Anthony Towns, one by Josh Hart) as the Knicks fell behind early in the first quarter. Midway through the period, Jalen Brunson scored seven in under two minutes to regain the lead. In this streaky contest, though, it made sense that he missed his next five attempts. Towns (12 PTS, 10 RBS, 7 AST, +21) was a game-time decision due to knee soreness, and his scoring showed the limp, producing a single point in the frame. Filling in for Anunoby in the first five, Landry Shamet made his sixth start of the season. He finished with a tidy 11 points on three made three-pointers.

The quarter was competitive until the final three minutes. After Pacôme Dadiet (yes, him!) drilled a triple to pull the Knicks within one, Cleveland rattled off a 10-0 run, powered by five consecutive points from Meleek Thomas, two Thomas Bryant free throws, and a Donovan Mitchell and-one. The latter easily had the biggest impact on the quarter, tallying 14 points, three dimes, and a steal. As for New York's bench support, Dadiet (7 PTS, 14 MIN) provided positive defensive efforts with a block and a steal, but neither Tyler Kolek nor Andre Drummond moved the needle. New York managed just six points over the final five-ish minutes, and Cleveland closed the quarter ahead, 34-23.

To start the second, the good guys outscored the villains by ten, chiseling an 11-point deficit to one. Credit Towns and his four points, two dimes, and two blocks for the comeback. Mohamed Diawara lent a triple, too, and Mikal Bridges found his rhythm, blasting out seven fast points. Naturally, Cleveland responded with a 9-2 stretch when James Harden (28 PTS, 1-8 3PT) finally got on the board. Dadiet drizzled water on our good feelings by missing three straight shots, and Brunson still couldn't regain his touch, going scoreless from the field in the period.

Playing from 55-44 down with four minutes left in the half, the hosts posted 11 unanswered points, eight of which belonged to Bridges. He'd finish the half with 17 points. This, my friends, is what's known as a circus shot:

Despite dealing with a sore shoulder, Hart picked Harden's pocket twice, converting one steal into a layup. Unfortunately, after Bridges tied the game at 55, Donovan Mitchell (30 PTS, 1-7 3PT) and Harden combined for six unanswered points, aided by a Bridges turnover. The home team won the quarter 33-27 but entered intermission trailing 61-56.

Through the half, Harden and Mitchell combined for 34 points in the half, while their bench contributed 13. New York had shot better from the field (54% to 49%) and significantly better from deep (50% to 31%), they were on the wrong end of the score due to a glaring free-throw disparity. New York had attempted just eight freebies, while Cleveland shot more than twice that. The Cavs outscored won the paint, 28-16, and the boards. Another glaring stat: Cleveland converted six Knicks miscues into 13 points, while New York squeezed just two points out of five giveaways.

Cleveland opened the second half on an 11-5 run to restore its double-digit lead. Down 74-65 midway through the period, the Knicks got a boost from an unexpected source: Tyler Nickel. The rookie sank a triple, followed by another from Landry Shamet, to cut the deficit to three. Nickel contributed five points but also committed four fouls in under six minutes—that will get straightened out with experience. Harden extended Cleveland's advantage to 85-77 with under three minutes remaining. But then another unexpected player pitched in: James Wiseman provided some valuable minutes off the bench, finishing the game with six points and seven boards in six minutes. Shamet helped the Knicks win the quarter 26-24 and enter the fourth trailing 85-82.

The Knicks opened the fourth quarter on a 12-0 run, turning a three-point deficit into a nine-point advantage in less than three minutes. Jose Alvarado (a team-high 21 points, 8-10 FG, 19 MIN) was the catalyst, drilling consecutive triples and picking Donovan Mitchell's pocket to set up a Dadiet layup. Hart took a turn orchestrating the offense, collecting four assists during the opening surge. The Ohio Players missed their first five shots, four of them by Mitchell, and committed two turnovers as the Knicks piled on the points.

Wiseman (!) helped goose New York's lead to 13 with four points and three offensive rebounds in a brief fourth-quarter stint. When Mitchell and Harden scored five unanswered points to trim the deficit to eight, Alvarado responded with a jumper, and Jalen Brunson buried a triple, his only basket of the quarter. Alvarado was the unquestionable star of the fourth, scoring 16 of his 21 points, including four three-pointers. Hart was nearly as impactful, contributing eight points, six assists, three rebounds, a steal, and a block in the frame. The Knicks closed on a 7-0 run, their defense holding Cleveland scoreless over the final two minutes and change.

Welcome back to associate head coach Chris Jent, who returned to the sidelines after a month away.

The Knicks hang around town to face the Heat on Friday in the last of their first-stage Cup games. Mo is folding and pressing your recap. Happy Knicksgiving, turkey fans.

#2964. Is Micron Stock a Buy After HPE Said Memory Will Stay Scarce Into 2028?

A big memory buyer and a bearish bank are now at least a year apart on when the shortage ends.

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 26, 2026 at 8:17AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Micron Technology ›

Hewlett Packard Enterprise (HPE +1.57%) told investors on Nov. 23 that it still can't get all the memory chips it wants. And it doesn't think that'll change soon.

"We expect supply of memory, NAND, and many other critical components to remain constrained to at least the second half of calendar 2028," CEO Antonio Neri said on the company's earnings call that evening.

That seems like good news for Micron Technology (MU -4.53%), which makes DRAM (the memory computers use while they work) and NAND flash storage chips. But the memory maker's shares slid anyway. Micron closed at $707.70 on Nov. 23, just before the call. As of this writing on Nov. 25, the stock traded near $550, less than half the $1,213.56 it closed at on June 25, its record.

Meanwhile, BNP Paribas downgraded the shares, saying memory supply might catch up to demand a year sooner than it had thought.

The tech firm said in March that DRAM and NAND make up over half the cost of the parts in a traditional server. And its inventory more than doubled in fiscal 2026, from around $6.4 billion on Oct. 31, 2025, to $11.8 billion by July 31 and $14.1 billion by Oct. 31, 2026.

HPE's timeline seems to have stretched, too. In March, Neri said he expected high DRAM and NAND prices to "persist well into 2027."

CFO Marie Myers said the $14.1 billion inventory reflected higher costs for commodity parts like memory, purchases for customer orders, and what she called "strategic buys." She added that HPE's commodity costs climbed by significant double-digit percentages in 2026, and she expects smaller increases ahead. But smaller increases are still increases, not the price drops a glut would cause.

BNP Paribas thinks one could. In a note reported on Nov. 24, it cut its rating on Micron to neutral from outperform. It now says DRAM and NAND supply and demand might approach balance starting in mid-2027 and drive average selling prices down. It flagged risks to big cloud companies' data center plans for 2028 and new factory capacity coming online.

Micron's stock dropped 11% that day, and again on Nov. 25, when research firm TrendForce said NAND flash might move into oversupply in 2027. TrendForce still expects DRAM to stay tight through 2027, and DRAM made up about three-quarters of Micron's revenue in fiscal 2026.

Part of the new factory capacity BNP mentioned is Micron's own. Its first new fab in Idaho should start making wafers in mid-2027, and a facility in Taiwan should begin meaningful shipments around then too. But Micron says output at a new fab ramps up over a few quarters. And its other planned fabs, including a second one in Idaho and a new NAND factory in Singapore, aren't set to start output until the second half of 2028 or later.

"[W]e do not have line of sight to when supply and demand will return to balance," management wrote about DRAM in Micron's prepared remarks for its Sept. 30 earnings call.

HPE's 2028 date matches Micron's building schedule. BNP's earlier date seems to rely more on demand weakening.

Micron's fiscal 2028 ends on Aug. 31, 2028, so HPE's outlook covers nearly all of the year. If the outlook holds, Micron could spend most of fiscal 2028 selling into a market still short on chips. If BNP is right, prices could be dropping for most of it.

The stock price looks closer to the second case.

At roughly $550, Micron trades at a price-to-earnings ratio of about 7.4 on its fiscal 2026 results. Based on the average analyst estimate for fiscal 2028, the price is below 3 times earnings.

Even BNP sees Micron's earnings per share bottoming at $86 in 2029, over the $74.33 per share Micron earned in fiscal 2026 under generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP). The stock looks cheap even on that bearish estimate.

One buyer's view could be wrong, of course. But I think HPE's outlook undercuts the glut worries, especially for DRAM, Micron's largest business. A buyer still stocking up at higher prices is tough to square with a stock priced for falling memory prices by mid-2027.

I'd consider buying some Micron shares here, in a position small enough to ride out more days like Nov. 24. If HPE starts pulling that 2028 date forward, like BNP just did with its own, I'd rethink that.

#2961. Forget the Market's Hottest Stocks. This Boring Dividend Stock Is Where I'd Put $1,000 Before 2027.

This unloved Dividend King isn't performing well right now, but history suggests it will get back on track.

By Reuben Gregg Brewer – Nov 26, 2026 at 8:32AM EST

Stock market volatility has ticked up dramatically in recent weeks, with the hottest stocks suddenly becoming some of the most volatile. If you are a long-term investor with a dividend focus and $1,000 to invest, you're better off taking a look at deeply unloved PepsiCo (PEP +0.88%). That will get you about seven shares.

There are good reasons for Wall Street to be downbeat about PepsiCo. However, the company's long and successful history suggests that investors who think in decades, not days, will be well rewarded. Here's why you should buy it before the start of 2027.

To get the bad news out of the way right up front, PepsiCo's business isn't doing particularly well right now. It isn't falling apart, either, but inflation, increasingly budget-conscious consumers, and shifts in consumer buying habits have taken a toll. In the third quarter of 2026, organic revenue growth was 3.1%. Coca-Cola (KO -0.03%), the company's primary competitor in the beverage space, saw organic sales rise more than twice as fast, at 7%, in the same quarter.

Simply put, investors are focusing on the industry leader and taking a show-me attitude with PepsiCo. You can see that in their valuations. For example, PepsiCo's dividend yield is 4.2% compared to Coca-Cola's 2.2%. Looking at more traditional valuation metrics, PepsiCo's price-to-sales ratio is just under 2x compared to a five-year average of 2.5x. Its price-to-earnings ratio is nearly 18x versus a five-year average of 25x. Coca-Cola's P/S ratio is 8x compared to a five-year average of 6.4x, and its P/E ratio is 28x compared to a five-year average of 26x. PepsiCo is cheap compared to its own history and relative to Coca-Cola.

That said, there are many reasons to like PepsiCo beyond valuation. For starters, as noted above, it isn't performing as well as Coca-Cola, but the company isn't doing poorly. It is probably best to view it as muddling through a difficult period. It has done that many times before over the last 54 years. If that seems oddly specific, it is. That's the number of years this Dividend King has increased its dividend. Sure, that's a decade less than Coca-Cola, but it is hard to complain about PepsiCo's streak.

Moreover, PepsiCo can stand toe-to-toe with Coca-Cola as a business. They are both industry leaders in brand strength, marketing prowess, innovation capabilities, and distribution. You aren't exactly stepping down to own PepsiCo. Meanwhile, PepsiCo offers you diversification that Coca-Cola does not. Coca-Cola is a beverage giant, but PepsiCo is a beverage giant (Pepsi), a snacking giant (Frito-Lay), and operates a large, well-respected packaged food business (Quaker Oats). For investors who value diversification, that may actually give it a leg up on Coca-Cola.

Meanwhile, it isn't like PepsiCo is sitting still and hoping conditions improve. It is actively working to shift its business in line with changes in consumer preferences. For example, it has acquired on-trend brands (Siete and Poppi), and it has been bringing out updated versions of existing brands, with a focus on healthier fare and protein. These are exactly the types of things that you would expect the company to do, but such moves can take time to play out.

If you are looking to buy a stock and see it go up the next day, PepsiCo won't be a good option for you. The turnaround could take a year or more. However, you'll be collecting an attractive 4.2% dividend yield backed by a Dividend King with an industry-leading business while you wait. For patient dividend investors, this could be a very compelling long-term opportunity.

#2960. Packers By The Numbers: Stats from the Week 12 loss to the Rams

As we do every week in this space, we're looking at some stats from the Green Bay Packers' most recent game, courtesy of SumerSports. Today we're looking at the 26-13 loss to the Los Angeles Rams in Week 12.

I don't have metrics on the broadcast, but I can tell you with some amount of certainty that there was less dead air in this game than any other game this season. Luke Kuechly brought some really nice insight into some of the plays, but my goodness, fellas, you're allowed to take a breath.

And stop trying to make "Thanksgiving Eve" happen. It's not going to happen.

After leaning into a heavy personnel grouping last week, this week saw the Packers go back to primarily playing out of 11 personnel (1 RB, 1 TE, 3 WR).

And, lest you think the numbers are skewed due to a small number of plays in the second half (and needing to catch up when they did have the ball), those full game numbers are roughly in line with the first half approach.

They were primarily a shotgun-based passing attack offense in this game. For the game, they were in shotgun on 84.1% of snaps and had a pass rate of 68.2%. Again, those numbers basically align with the 1st half. They were 78.6% shotgun and 64.3% pass in the 1st half.

As we've seen them do all year, they switched up their run game approach in this game. After going with a power-heavy approach against the Vikings, they were back to a zone-based attack this week.

Inside Zone didn't work overly well for them, but they did well with Outside Zone and Power. I'm curious to dig a little deeper into that when the film hits. Was that a matter of a smaller sample size, or was that something they could have leaned a bit heavier into if they wanted to?

Jordan Love had a pretty rough day in the stat sheet. He went 17/29 for 198 yards, 1 TD and 2 INTs for an EPA per Play of -0.31. That would go down as his worst game of the year by EPA per Play (behind the -0.22 he posted against the Vikings last week). As I mentioned at the top, I thought Kuechly did a nice job breaking down what Love was expecting on that 1st INT, and the second INT certainly seemed to be a case where Bo Melton cut off his route. So there are certainly things to consider (and things we'll certainly be breaking down this week), but, right now, the numbers are the numbers and the numbers are bad.

Love did seem to have a little more time to throw in this game, but, as with every game this season, time is relative. The average time to pressure against Love was 2.3 seconds, 0.26 seconds below the NFL average. His average time to throw of 2.62 seconds seems downright functional, and would make this his 5th longest time to throw in a game this year. It's still 0.08 seconds below the NFL average. So protection was better, but still below average. Still, it felt functional in a way it certainly did not against the Vikings.

Love came into this game shredding Man coverage, and the Rams came into the game playing one of the highest rates of man coverage in the league. Love continued playing well against man, but the zone stuff is ugly.

We don't know how long Christian Watson will be out, but it sure would be nice if Tucker Kraft could find his game a little bit. He clearly is not all the way back from his injury (whether that's physical or mental, I don't really know), but the offense won't really have a chance if he can't find a way to turn it on.

Micah Parsons played limited snaps in his return to the field, but he was impactful while he was there, notching a 21.4% pressure rate and an average time to pressure of 2.47 seconds.

Parsons posted a pressure rate of 20.2% and an average time to pressure of 2.76 seconds last season, so he looked like Micah Parsons while he was on the field. That was certainly encouraging.

With Parsons back, I was certainly hoping for more from Lukas Van Ness, but we didn't really see that. He had a pressure rate of 6.7% and an average time to pressure of 2.6 seconds. With as run-heavy as the Rams were (66.2% run rate for the game, 81.4% in the 2nd half) it's hard to glean too much in terms of how the pass rush will look with Parsons back. Something to keep an eye on going forward.

Chris McClellan turned in a nice game with 4 assisted tackles, 4 solo tackles and 1 tackle for loss. I'm excited to take a look at him when the film comes out.

Albums listened to: Jay-Z – The Blueprint 2: The Gift & The Curse

#2954. President Donald Trump Considers Jensen Huang's Return to Nvidia a "Great Decision," but It Doesn't Resolve a Critical Problem

Jensen Huang and Nvidia’s board committed the cardinal sin of investing.

By Sean Williams – Nov 26, 2026 at 9:06AM EST

It's been a wild couple of weeks for Nvidia (NVDA -3.91%), its shareholders, and Wall Street as a whole, to put it mildly.

Following the admission of an affair with an alleged Chinese spy, as well as allegations of two extramarital affairs with employees, Huang resigned as CEO on Nov. 12, spurring an elevator-down move in his company's stock the likes of which have never been witnessed. Over 45 calendar days (ending Nov. 20), Nvidia lost $2.75 trillion in market value.

Jensen Huang and Nvidia's board still have a big problem on their hands. Image source: Official White House Photo by Joyce N. Boghosian.

Then came the 180 on Monday, Nov. 23, with Nvidia's board reversing course and reinstating Huang as CEO after just 11 days. President Donald Trump congratulated the board on "what I consider to be an absolutely great decision," with a White House spokesperson claiming the president was involved in Huang's reinstatement as CEO.

While Huang's return does resolve some near-term concerns for Nvidia, a critical problem remains that won't be easy to tackle.

Nvidia didn't add more than $900 billion in market cap on Monday by accident. Huang has become the face of the artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure build-out, and his presence keeps Nvidia at the center of this multitrillion-dollar revolution.

“The NVIDIA Board of Directors has made the correct and proper decision to return Jensen Huang to the position of CEO… Congratulations to Jensen, the NVIDIA Board, to the People of the United States — and, indeed, the World!” - President DONALD J. TRUMP pic.twitter.com/bKAxuMr9aA

Jensen Huang leading the charge also means that Nvidia's aggressive innovation timeline stays on track. The company intends to bring an advanced AI chip to market annually, securing its top spot in graphics processing unit (GPU) compute capabilities.

Perhaps most importantly, questions about the numerous circular financing deals Huang orchestrated are likely to die down. Nvidia has pledged jaw-dropping amounts of capital to neoclouds, hyperscalers, and large language model developers who, in turn, will purchase GPUs from the company.

Huang's return, coupled with Nvidia's eye-popping growth forecast, suggests its stock is a bargain -- but that's not the full story.

Jensen Huang's biggest challenge awaits. Image source: Getty Images.

Although Wall Street cheered this reinstatement, Nvidia committed the ultimate investing no-no: it broke investors' trust.

Among billionaire Warren Buffett's laundry list of investing quotes, he once stated that "It takes 20 years to build a reputation and five minutes to ruin it." This month, we've had Nvidia's CEO and co-founder admit to an extramarital affair, have seen several probes kicked off by regulators into Nvidia's accounting and operating practices, and watched the company's board flip-flop on the company's longtime boss over a two-week stretch.

What investor trust had been engendered has arguably been lost or damaged, and there's no quick fix for rebuilding it.

This intangible damage over the last couple of weeks may slow future circular financing deals, even with Jensen Huang back at the helm, and could further contract Nvidia's already historically low earnings multiple. Until Nvidia rebuilds trust with Wall Street and investors -- and there's no set timeline for this -- the upside for its stock and its dealmaking capacity may be capped.

#2952. Investing $500 a Month in Coca-Cola, Realty Income, and Altria Could Generate Over $1,000 in Monthly Dividend Income by 2046

As the old adage goes, slow and steady wins the race. And as another adage reminds us, quality pays for itself.

By James Brumley – Nov 26, 2026 at 9:31AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Coca-Cola ›

Will you need dividend income at some point in the future? For most people who plan on eventually retiring from the world of work, the answer is yes. They're growing their nest eggs in the meantime, in fact, largely with the intention of converting their growth investments into income investments when the time comes.

That isn't necessarily the only way to meet your future income needs, though. As difficult as it may be to believe, there's an argument to be made for beginning to build your dividend-paying positions right now, even if you don't need the income yet.

And while nobody can perfectly predict the future, your most promising dividend prospects for this purpose are Coca-Cola (KO -0.03%), Realty Income (O +1.18%), and Altria Group (MO +1.95%).

These names are among the market's most reliable and highest-yielding dividend payers, and each has raised its annualized dividend for at least 50 consecutive years, reflecting the resiliency of their underlying businesses. That makes all three of them Dividend Kings.

Their dividend pedigrees aren't as important as the process, however. How does one turn a $500 monthly investment in these names into a portfolio capable of producing more than $1,000 in monthly dividend income by, say, 20 years from now, in 2046?

The first is the growth phase. Rather than pocketing the dividends these stocks will be paying in the coming two decades, you'll be reinvesting these payments into more shares of the ticker while also purchasing more of these same stocks with your monthly contributions of $500.

This is no minor growth driver. Over the past 20 years, dividend reinvestment has essentially doubled the effective net returns each of these stocks would have achieved with price appreciation alone.

The other aspect of the first stage is a bit trickier to get a handle on. That's figuring out the likely net returns these three names will achieve between now and 2046. Although past performance doesn't guarantee future results, experienced investors know that it's a pretty good indicator of what's likely to happen.

Nevertheless, given the modest but steady traction the smoking-cessation movement is getting, it seems unlikely that tobacco giant Altria will deliver a repeat performance of the past two decades in the next two. For that matter, Coca-Cola's net gains since 2006 have also been exaggerated.

So, for the sake of simplicity and plausibility, let's just assume all three of these stocks have and will continue to at least match the overall market's average annual net gain of 10%. (Each has beaten this figure, actually.) Investing $500 per month in any instruments achieving this sort of annual return for 20 years would leave you with a portfolio worth a little more than $380,000 at the end of this time frame.

And again, just for the sake of simplicity, let's assume this $380,000 nest egg is evenly allocated between Altria, Realty Income, and Coca-Cola. Those are positions of roughly $127,000 each. How much dividend income would these collective investments be producing then?

We're still required to make some assumptions, albeit not unreasonable ones. Namely, let's assume these stocks' current dividend yields will more or less be their yields at that time. That's 2.2% for Coca-Cola, 5.6% for Realty Income, and 6% for Altria. Based on those numbers, this $380,000 in stock holdings should generate over $17,000 in dividends every year, or more than $1,400 in monthly spendable income.

Just don't forget to turn your dividend reinvestment plan off when you're ready to start collecting these dividend payments in cash. That's the second stage of the process.

These results are just hypothetical, of course, although the assumptions are rooted in real-world numbers that should be roughly repeatable.

The big takeaway isn't identifying these three dividend payers as your top future income prospects, although there's little doubt that these companies will still be experiencing solid demand for their goods and services into the foreseeable future.

No, the real takeaway is just how much even small regular investments in dividend stocks, paired with a dividend reinvestment plan, can grow your portfolio over time. This strategy can yield growth-like returns — the key is just giving it enough time to do the job. Note that most of the net growth for all three of these positions materialized in just the last one-third of the 20-year accumulation period.

The other related takeaway is that even though it may be years down the road for you, it wouldn't be wrong to start thinking about — and even building — positions in the dividend-paying stocks that you know you'll want to own as your income-generating positions then. Although dividends paid by stocks not held in retirement accounts are taxable as income for the year in which they're paid, that may still be a smarter option for you than buying growth investments that will result in large capital gains tax bills when you're ready to convert them into income-generating holdings.

#2951. Nets topple Bucks in NBA Cup clash to snap five-game skid

The teams with the two worst records in the Eastern Conference faced each other Wednesday night in Milwaukee, but no one needed to accuse either the Nets or the Bucks of tanking in this one.

Neither team can win the draft lottery after the season because both franchises essentially traded their respective 2027 first-round picks years ago, but the Nets at least halted their five-game losing streak with a defensively improved 104-97 victory in an NBA Cup game at Fiserv Forum.

"Just do it again. Every time we have a good performance where we fight and we compete, we follow it up with some bulls–t, with some bad ones," Noah Clowney told the YES Network after the game. "So we're trying to stack it this time and keep two or three good ones in a row."

Julius Randle netted a team-high 28 points as the Nets (4-13) posted their first NBA Cup victory after losses earlier in the season against the Pistons and the Magic.

Julius Randle, who scored a team-high 28 points, shoots a floater during the Nets' 104-97 win over the Bucks on Nov. 25, 2026, in Milwaukee. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

Michael Porter Jr. was held to 12 points on 4-for-14 shooting with 11 rebounds to follow another quiet scoring night (11) Monday in Minnesota.

Egor Dëmin had an active game with 15 points and a plus-15 rating in 25 minutes off the bench.

Clowney nailed three 3-pointers among his 17 points, and Day'Ron Sharpe contributed 11 points, 14 boards and five assists for the Nets.

Tyler Herro, who had a career-best 50-point game Saturday against the 76ers, scored 21 of his 28 points in the second half for the Bucks (2-17).

"We've lost a few in a row, so we've gotta try to get a win no matter who we're playing against," Porter told YES before the game. "They're an NBA team. They are at the bottom of the standings right now, but so are we, so it's gonna be a dogfight, and we gotta try to get out of here with a win."

The NBA instituted rule changes in the offseason to discourage tanking for draft position, essentially penalizing the teams with the worst three records in the league with lower odds to secure the No. 1 overall pick in the lottery.

The Nets' 2027 first-round pick is controlled by the Rockets from an unprotected pick swap as part of Brooklyn's deal to acquire James Harden in January 2021.

The Bucks, who dealt away superstar forward Giannis Antetokounmpo to the Heat over the summer, also had committed to a pick swap involving their 2027 first-rounder when they acquired guard Jrue Holiday from New Orleans in 2020.

Noah Clowney, who scored 17 points off the bench, puts up a shot during the second quarter of the Nets' road win over the Bucks. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

The Nets had allowed at least 70 points in the first half four times during their five-game skid, but this slow start was more due to offensive inefficiency.

Brooklyn missed five of its first six shots and spotted the Bucks a 9-2 lead in the first three minutes.

But Porter knocked down two straight 3s as part of a 15-1 run en route to a 28-24 edge through 12 minutes.

A 3-pointer by Clowney and a jumper by Randle extended the cushion to 10 early in the second. The Nets led by as many as 13 before the Bucks closed within 51-47 before intermission.

"We have to close quarters better," Randle, who had 16 in the half, told YES.

Rookie point guard Mikel Brown Jr. scored all six of his points in the second half after posting 17 in his first NBA start Monday in Minnesota.

Brown finally recorded his first two buckets after seven misses to push Brooklyn back ahead before two late dunks by Sharpe and a traditional 3-point play by Dëmin helped the Nets carry a 77-71 edge into the final quarter.

"It all came from the defensive side. I was more locked in, and everything else came to me naturally," Dëmin said.

Day'Ron Sharpe, who scored 11 points and grabbed 14 rebounds, slams home a dunk during the Nets' win over the Bucks in Milwaukee. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

Keon Ellis (plus-14) and Grant Nelson provided some defensive energy off the bench to open the fourth, and Porter converted an and-1 to stretch the lead back to 11 with 10:38 remaining.

"Overall, proud of the effort. Our guys played hard," Nets coach Jordi Fernández said. "Guys were asking for subs, and that tells me that everybody did everything possible.

"That's how it's supposed to be … and that's who we want to be. Now let's see if we can play this hard two games in a row."

Moritz Wagner didn't play in the second half due to what the Nets said was left knee soreness.

There were many questions as to who Almada decided to start in the game. The Uruguayan decided to bench Luis Angel Malagon, Henry Martin and Carlos Alvarez just to name a few. Defender Ramon Juárez was even left off the roster even though he has proven himself time and time again. Even with an advantage for the majority of the game the Aguilas were unable to score. This was a scenario where they should have scored a minimum of three goals. Instead they struggled in the final third and at times it looked as if they were lost and were unsure of what they were supposed to do. They will head back home for the second leg where all they have to do is draw to move forward as they are the higher seed. However, if they want to make it to the final they need to come up with a plan or they will once again find themselves eliminated in the playoffs.

#2949. OMS Energy (OMSE) Q2 2027 Earnings Call Transcript

Saudi order delays, record cash and a services shift shape this quarter's results.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 26, 2026 at 9:53AM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

OMS Energy (OMSE +6.67%) reported a decrease in revenue for the first half of fiscal year 2027 but expanded its gross margin while maintaining a debt-free balance sheet with record cash reserves. Management attributed the revenue decline primarily to procurement delays in Saudi Arabia, where orders under a long-term supply agreement are being deferred instead of canceled. The company is actively diversifying its geographic footprint with new orders in Malaysia, Kuwait, and Pakistan while shifting toward a higher-margin services revenue mix. Strategic efforts focus on cost discipline and cash generation to manage through extended customer decision cycles.

Operator: Hello, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you for joining OMS Energy Technologies, Inc.'s Earnings Presentation for the first half of fiscal year 2027. Today's presentation is being recorded. The company's financial and operating results for the 6 months ended September 30, 2026, have been released and are available on the OMS Investor Relations website at ir.omsos.com. Please note that today's discussion will contain forward-looking statements made under the safe harbor provisions of the U.S. Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995. These statements typically contain words such as may, will, expect, target, estimate, intend, believe, potential, continue, or similar expressions. Forward-looking statements involve risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially from those projected or anticipated.

These include changes in customer spending and procurement, the timing of call-off orders and deliveries, geopolitical developments, supply chain conditions, and foreign exchange movements. Further information about risks is included in the company's filings with the SEC. The company does not undertake any obligation to update forward-looking information, except as required under applicable law. Today's discussion also includes adjusted EBITDA and adjusted free cash flow, which are supplemental non-IFRS measures. Their definitions and reconciliations to the most directly comparable IFRS measures are included in the earnings release. I will now turn the presentation over to Mr. Meng Hock How. Please go ahead.

Meng Hock How: Thank you, operator, and thank you to everyone joining us today. I am Meng Hock How, CEO of OMS Energy Technologies. I'll discuss our first half performance, what we're seeing in customer orders and the progress we're making across our business. I will then address several questions that we expect investors to have about the results. For investors newer to OMS, we are Singapore headquartered equipment manufacturer and engineered solutions supplier serving upstream oil and gas operators. Our core products include Surface Wellhead and Christmas Tree Systems, Specialty Connectors and Pipe. We also provide premium threading, repair, and other engineering services through 11 manufacturing facilities across 6 markets in Asia Pacific, Middle East, and North Africa. Turning to our results.

I'm pleased to report that we expanded our gross margin, generated strong cash flow and further strengthened our debt-free balance sheet in the first half. Gross margin increased to 31.4% from 28.2% in the prior year period, and net profit margin improved to 18.8% from 17.6%. We generated $25.3 million in operating cash flow and approximately twice our net profit and ended September with a record $172.3 million in cash, cash equivalents, and restricted cash or approximately $4.06 per share. We delivered these results while macro conditions remain challenging given ongoing geopolitical uncertainty and supply chain disruption in our major markets. Our facilities remained operational throughout the period.

Customers continued to tender and place orders with OMS during the half, but took longer to commit and schedule deliveries, weighing on revenue. First half revenue was $66.7 million compared with $82.8 million a year ago, largely due to the slower pace of Saudi Aramco call-off orders. This was partially offset by increased activity in Malaysia and Thailand, showing the value of our diversified footprint. In the current environment, the challenge for OMS lies not in lack of opportunities, but in reduced visibility, extended decision cycle and execution timing. You will see that pattern throughout this period's result.

With order timing harder to predict, we are concentrating on factors that we can control, including careful management of inventory, costs, and customer deliveries. By keeping inventory, purchasing and costs in line with the orders we actually received, we were able to protect profitability and generate strong cash flow despite fluctuating order volumes. Saudi Arabia is where reduced visibility and order timing have affected us most. Revenue from Specialty Connectors and Pipe was $37.1 million compared with $51.1 million a year ago as the timing of the order placed under our long-term supply agreement with Saudi Aramco continued to slow. I would like to emphasize, as previously mentioned, that these are deferred orders, not lost orders.

Our agreement with Saudi Aramco remains intact. Our relationship remains strong, and there has been no cancellation. To that end, we continue to receive business from Aramco. In September, we announced a $7.1 million order for specialty connectors and pipe won through an open tender outside our long-term agreement. Since that award, we have received a call-off order of approximately $3 million under the long-term agreement as well as a separate $500,000 order for loose connectors for Aramco's float equipment. We view these awards as a reflection of Aramco's confidence in our product and the delivery record we have built in the Kingdom over 15 years.

In Indonesia, our operation in Bogor just received an order for specialty pipe and connectors valued at approximately $396,000 for Pertamina North West Java. This is a follow-on from the order we announced in January 2026. Furthermore, our operations in Singapore and Malaysia secured their first order for specialty connectors and pipe for Petronas in Malaysia, valued at approximately $687,000. Of that amount, $367,000 was delivered during the first half of fiscal 2027. We also continue to bid for supply contracts with Kuwait Oil Company, KOC, and are actively engaged in the tender process we have entered after we qualified for the approved vendor list in July.

Also following that qualification, we received a $458,000 order from Tenaris for OMS connectors that we welded onto Tenaris pipe and supplied to Kuwait Oil Company as part of Tenaris OCTG package. While modest in size, the Petronas and Tenaris order extend our specialty connectors and pipe business to 2 more national oil companies, which we believe can lead to additional business over time. We've also recently bid to supply specialty connectors and Pipe for a development in the South China Sea with results expected next month. If we are successful, this will mark our reentry into China after more than 10 years and represent further progress in our ongoing geographic diversification efforts.

In surface wellhead and Christmas trees, we continue to win orders while revenue follows customer and project schedules. First half revenue from Surface Wellhead and Christmas Tree was $3.5 million compared to $6.2 million a year ago. Indonesia is the main market for this business and upstream activities there has been slow over the past year as new oil and gas projects have taken longer to come online. In September, we announced $2.3 million order from Pertamina Hulu Rokan under our existing 3-year supply contract following the contract extension we announced in March. We also recently received $766,000 order from Pertamina for choke valves, a component of Christmas tree systems.

Separately, we are awaiting a call-off of approximately $1 million from Pertamina under an existing contract. We also completed the stackup and function test of a new compact surface wellhead and Christmas tree system for Pertamina, a milestone in developing our wellhead product offering with an established customer. The equipment has been delivered and is being installed by Pertamina on its Tamadun-001 field, where it is carrying out an unconventional drilling program. Indonesia holds vast unconventional oil and gas resources, including shale gas, coal-bed methane, methane hydrate, and shale oil, and commercial development is still at an early stage. We believe this installation position us well as that activity develops.

OMS Singapore has also just delivered a complete 10,000 psi wellhead and Christmas tree system to Orient Petroleum in Pakistan, marking OMS Singapore's first delivery of such a system to the country. Orient Petroleum is one of Pakistan's largest private exploration and production company and has been operating since 1979. OMS Singapore has also received a supply contract for 2 sets of production tree for a Bleiswijk geothermal cluster in Netherlands. While the value is small at approximately $130,000, it is a step into the European energy sector that we want to build on. Across our service businesses, customers also continued to place orders during the half.

Other ancillary services revenue rose to $9.4 million from $7.5 million, an increase of approximately 25%, supported by repair, refurbishment, and engineering work in Thailand and Malaysia. This maintenance work depends less on new project approval than equipment sales do. Premium threading services revenue was $16.7 million compared to $18 million. Together, our services business accounted for approximately 39% of first half revenue, up from approximately 31% a year ago. In Singapore, we've also secured an approximately $0.55 million of repair work on production risers for a large oilfield services company for the upcoming Kikeh 3B Phase 2 subsea project in Malaysia. The Kikeh field is operated by PTTEP Malaysia. Our backlog reflects the period's slower order timing pattern.

At September 30, it stood at $40.9 million compared to $60.7 million at March 31, primarily due to call-off timing under the Saudi agreement. Our backlog comprises confirmed orders that have not yet been recognized as revenue and are expected to be delivered within the following 12 months. It does not include orders received after September 30, including the additional Saudi Aramco order I mentioned earlier. A framework agreement, a tender opportunity, and a confirmed call-off each represent different stages of the customer's procurement process and the backlog captures only the last of those stages. Today, opportunities are taking longer to move from tender to award and from framework agreement to call-off.

To put the backlog figure in context, Saudi Aramco maintained its 2026 capital spending guidance of $50 billion to $55 billion when it reported third quarter's results this month. Aramco attributed lower upstream spending in the quarter, mainly to the timing of crude oil increment projects and several of its major upstream developments, including the Dammam development and the second phase of Jafurah are scheduled to come on stream in 2027. Across the wider region, BMI expects oil and gas capital spending to grow about 4% this year and to average approximately 6% a year from 2027 to 2030. Now let me walk through our financial results in more detail.

First half revenue was $66.7 million compared to $82.8 million in the prior year period. Gross profit was $21 million compared to $23.3 million. Gross margin increased from 28.2% to 31.4%, supported by a more favorable mix in Saudi Specialty connectors and pipe and a greater contribution from higher-margin ancillary services in Thailand. Selling and general and administrative expenses were $7.1 million compared to $5.4 million in the prior year period. The increase primarily reflected personnel costs and professional services, governance, and compliance-related fees. Operating profit was $13.9 million compared to $17.9 million, and operating margin was 20.8% compared to 21.6%. Finance income increased to $2.4 million from $1.6 million, reflecting more cash placed in interest-earning deposits.

Income tax expense was $3.6 million compared with $4.6 million, and our effective tax rate was 22.3% compared to 24%. Net profit was $12.6 million compared with $14.6 million, and net profit margin increased to 18.8% from 17.6%. Basic and diluted earnings per share were $0.28 compared with $0.33, reflecting lower profit attributable to shareholders and a higher weighted average share count. Adjusted EBITDA was $17.3 million compared to $21.8 million. Adjusted EBITDA margin held relatively steady at 25.9% compared with 26.4% despite the 19% decline in revenue. We continue to use this supplement measure alongside the IFRS results. The earnings release sets out the adjustment and the reconciliation from net profit. Cash generation remained an area of strength.

Operating cash flow was $25.3 million compared with $26.4 million. Adjusted free cash flow was $19.9 million compared with $25.4 million. As described in our earnings release, this measure now deducts cash placed in fixed rate structured deposits, and the change from last year mainly reflects the $5 million we placed in these deposits during the period. Part of our cash generation came from a reduction in inventory, which contributed $9.5 million to operating cash flow compared to $12.2 million a year ago. Inventory ended September at $7.7 million compared to $17.2 million at March 31. The level of inventory and the timing of replenishment will affect cash flow as order activity changes.

We will continue to manage purchasing and production against the equipment needed for customer deliveries. Net cash used in investing activities was $5.4 million. This included $5 million placed in fixed rate structured deposits and approximately $0.4 million for property, plant, and equipment and software. These deposits are not included in our reported cash balance of $172.3 million. Cash and cash equivalents and restricted cash increased to a record $172.3 million at September 30 compared with $104.3 million at March 31. Our balance sheet remains free of bank borrowings. We continue to refine our capital allocation plan and assess investment opportunities while maintaining the financial flexibility to meet delivery commitments.

Looking ahead, our immediate planning reflects the slow pace of Saudi call-off and the orders we have in hand across the business. The September Aramco tender win and the Pertamina order were announced with expected delivery dates in January and March 2027, respectively. We are focused on execution against the schedule and continued engagement with our customers. We remain cautious about the timing of a wider recovery in procurement. At the same time, we are encouraged by our recent order activity, the growing contribution from our services business and the opportunities we are pursuing across the Middle East and Asia.

We have long-term agreement with leading national oil companies, 11 manufacturing facilities close to our customers and more than $172 million in cash with no debt. We believe this strength positions OMS well to capture future opportunities. Before we close, I would like to address questions investors are likely to have in the half, including how current visibility compares with the expectation that we discussed in June. Question one, how much of the earnings decline is attributable to geopolitical tensions and have projects been canceled? We have not observed material projects cancellation directly attributable to geopolitical development. The effect has been indirect primarily through longer customer decision cycle, deferred projects award and increased caution in customer capital spending decisions.

For OMS, the main operating issue has been the slower pace of Saudi call-off orders. Some customers have taken longer to finalize investment decisions or have adjusted project time lines, and it is difficult to isolate a specific financial impact. Opportunities remain in place, but decision and awards are taking longer. We are actively pursuing work in Malaysia, Indonesia, the UAE, Kuwait and China. Question two, is demand weakening, particularly in the Middle East? We would characterize the environment as one of increased caution rather than broad-based demand weakness. Activity levels vary by customers and markets. Customers continue to evaluate opportunities and pursue strategic projects, although decision-making and approval processes have become more measured.

In certain cases, this has translated into longer approval and procurement cycles. For OMS, that shows up as a slower Saudi call-off and a longer gap between tender and award. Several of the largest oilfield services have also pointed to the limited near-term visibility in the Middle East in their recent results. Even so, Saudi Aramco has maintained its 2026 capital spending guidance and has continued to award us business, including the $7.1 million tender award in September and additional orders since then. Our focus remains on supporting customers while adapting to changing market conditions. Third question, have supply chain disruption become a major concern? Supply chain conditions remain manageable, but less predictable than in previous period.

We have experienced longer lead time and occasional logistic challenges affecting certain projects. Management continues to work closely with suppliers and customers to mitigate these impacts where possible. The fourth question, how are margins holding up? And can they be sustained? Well, first half gross margin increased to 31.4% from 28.2%, supported by the mix of equipment specification and services. Operating margin was 20.8%. We continue to manage procurement, project execution and operating costs carefully. Our priority is maintaining financial discipline while remaining competitive in the markets we serve. However, if activity slows further in the second half, margins could be affected. We will continue to manage costs carefully as conditions evolve.

Question five, do we expect conditions to improve in the second half? Given the uncertain geopolitical and macroeconomic environment, we do not believe it will be appropriate to speculate on the timing or extent of any improvement. Customers continue to tender and place orders with OMS. Improvement depends on visibility returning and decision cycles shortening, and we do not control that timing. Continued uncertainty could further influence customer spending, project timing and supply chain performance. We continue to maintain long-standing customer relationships, technical capabilities and a diversified market presence. Management remains focused on factors within our control, including customer engagement, operational execution, cost management, and cash generation.

Sixth question is, does the expectation of modest FY 2027 revenue growth discussed in June still hold? Well, in June, we expected fiscal 2027 revenue to be modestly above fiscal 2026, assuming no further material geopolitical disruption. Since then, the Saudi call-off activity has remained slow and the timing of a broader pickup remains uncertain. Given the first half revenue of $66.7 million and the continued slow pace of call-off, we no longer expect fiscal 2027 revenue to exceed fiscal 2026. We're not providing a specific range today, and we will update investors as visibility on order timing improves. The last point or question 7, how do you plan to use your cash?

Well, our priorities are consistent with what we shared in June. First, we are reinvesting in core businesses, including capacity, certification, and in-region capabilities that support our growth. Second, we continue to review bolt-on acquisition opportunities with discipline and our cash position allows us to pursue them without raising new capital or taking on debt. We do not currently have a dividend and buyback program in place, but our balance sheet gives us the flexibility to introduce one when timing and conditions are right. We continue to refine our capital allocation plan, and we'll update investors as it develops. With this, we conclude our prepared remarks. Thank you very much for joining us today.

Operator: This concludes today's conference call. Thank you all for participating. You may now disconnect your lines. Thank you.

#2947. Anthropic Is Down 7% Since Its IPO. History Says a $5,000 Investment Could Be Worth This Much in a Year.

By Jennifer Saibil – Nov 26, 2026 at 10:13AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

Anthropic (ANT +1.08%) made history last week when it went public in the largest initial public offering (IPO) ever. But so far, the stock isn't moving in the direction shareholders would hope for. The stock, which went public at $83 per share, opened its first day of trading at $111.50, and closed it at $97.17, has fallen to $77.40 as of this writing. That’s a 7% drop from the IPO price, and a much steeper one from the prices that retail investors could get on Day 1.

But things can change quickly in the stock market, and a year from now, Anthropic could be much higher -- or much lower. Let's consider what a $5,000 investment in Anthropic stock might be worth in a year.

The theory behind the bull case for Anthropic is that its Claude artificial intelligence (AI) model will be integrated into businesses everywhere. As of now, it already has 11,900 customers paying it at least $100,000 annually, including 250 customers with annual spending of more than $10 million. Widespread enterprise adoption of Claude could lead to a state where it's simply viewed as a necessary part of business operations; that would generate incredible growth at Anthropic.

The company’s most recent results have been phenomenal. Trailing-12-month revenue increased 1,200% year over year in the third quarter, and if it continues to demonstrate such staggering growth, it could quickly become one of the largest companies in the world by sales. It would also become profitable at scale, and its vast investments in compute power — which it has been gobbling up from a roster of top AI infrastructure providers such as Amazon (AMZN -3.12%), Alphabet (GOOG -2.02%)(GOOGL -2.13%), and Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +1.30%)— would yield results. It has spending agreements with these companies and others to the tune of around $900 billion, with deals extending into 2036.

Management sees vast use cases for its technology. It envisions AI helping to end poverty and cure disease as well as drive economic growth.

AI is definitely heating up, but it may not offer long-term growth in quite the way Anthropic imagines. To begin with, there are already many competitors offering foundation models, and as the technology changes, there could be more. The market is already forgetting how AI stocks tumbled when the Chinese-developed model Deepseek came onto the scene last year. There's no reason to believe other things like that can't happen. Anthropic itself is an extremely young company, and it only launched Claude in 2023, pulling market share from OpenAI's ChatGPT.

IPO stocks tend to have bumpy rides early on, and given Anthropic's high price-to-sales multiple of 29, the stock has considerable room to drop if the company’s growth slows down. Wall Street expects its revenue to increase by 137% in 2027 — but that would be a major deceleration. If that forecast proves accurate, the market may conclude that Anthropic isn’t worth such a lofty sales multiple. If its top-line growth meets Wall Street's expectations, revenue would be about $83 billion. But if its P/S ratio gets cut in half to 14, its market cap would be $1.16 trillion. That's a 16% increase from today's value, turning a $5,000 investment made now into a position worth about $5,800.

#2946. Rocket Lab Stock Just Fell to Its Lowest Price in About a Year. Is It a Buy Now?

Shares are back where they were a year ago, but the business behind them is much bigger.

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 26, 2026 at 10:23AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Rocket Lab ›

Rocket Lab (RKLB +2.26%) stock fell about 7% on Wednesday, Nov. 25, to roughly $43 as I write. The drop left shares at their lowest price since early December 2025 and over 70% below their May 27 record close of $150.23.

The growth stock had closed above $66 just two weeks before, soon after Rocket Lab's third-quarter results, and about a third of that value has vanished since.

But the business behind the stock is far bigger than it was the last time shares traded this low. What hasn't changed is that Neutron, the reusable rocket behind Rocket Lab's long-term growth plans, hasn't yet flown.

The last time Rocket Lab stock traded this low, in early December 2025, the company's revenue for the previous four quarters came to around $555 million. For the last four quarters, it's about $882 million, almost 60% more. Growth has sped up, too. Revenue for the third quarter of 2026 climbed 73% from a year earlier to a record $268 million, after growing 36% in the fourth quarter of 2025. And management expects fourth-quarter revenue of $270 million to $290 million, which at the midpoint would be 56% above the year-ago quarter -- still fast, though slower than the third quarter's rate.

The share count grew, too, but far more slowly. Rocket Lab sold around $3.9 billion in new stock through September, partly to pay for its pending purchase of satellite operator Iridium Communications. Counting preferred stock that converts to common shares, it had about 15% more shares at the end of September than at the end of 2025.

That leaves much more revenue behind each share. At roughly $43, Rocket Lab is valued at about $29 billion, which comes to 33 times the revenue it took in over the last year. At the same share price in early December 2025, the stock traded at more than 40 times sales.

Still, 33 times sales is a high valuation for a business that isn't profitable yet, and I think much of that price depends on Neutron.

Rocket Lab now has 16 Neutron missions booked before the rocket's first flight, and half of them were signed in the third quarter alone, for over $515 million.

But at least six of those eight new missions aren't scheduled until late 2028 or later. Radar-satellite company ICEYE's three launches start in late 2028, Muon Space's two are set for 2029 and 2030, and Starcloud's is booked for 2029.

Rocket Lab expects only around a third of its $3.6 billion total backlog (signed work it hasn't yet counted as revenue) to become revenue within 12 months. Neutron's far-off missions likely make up a big part of the rest.

Neutron's first launch, meanwhile, is expected in 2027. Before Rocket Lab picks a specific date, it still needs to put together the rocket's first-stage tank and finish pressure and cryogenic (super-cold) testing, according to its last quarterly report.

Neutron has to fly first, and even then its early missions might not make money. In the third quarter, Rocket Lab recorded a forward loss reserve (an expected loss booked ahead of time) on the vehicle for Neutron's second flight, because the rocket is still so early in development.

Until Neutron flies regularly, the losses will likely keep coming. Rocket Lab lost $18 million in the third quarter on a non-GAAP (adjusted) basis, measured by earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA). That was smaller than its $26 million loss a year earlier, and management expects another loss of $14 million to $20 million in the fourth quarter.

On the third-quarter earnings call, CFO Adam Spice said capital spending should stay high as Neutron heads toward production, but he expects adjusted EBITDA to go positive within six months of the rocket's first successful test flight.

I think it can be, for investors willing to wait for Neutron. Each share now comes with much more revenue than it did at this price a year ago, and customers keep booking Neutron launches. But Neutron still has to show it can fly, then fly often. And a stock that shed a third of its value over two weeks might swing just as hard again. I'd consider buying in stages instead of all at once.

#2943. Rapid Recap: Nets 104, Bucks 97

The Milwaukee Bucks follow a string of solid games with one of their worst in a long time, losing at home to the Brooklyn Nets, 104-97. Tyler Herro led the Bucks with 28 points and five assists, while Kel'el Ware led the Bucks bench with 13 points and 14 rebounds. Julius Randle led the Nets with 28 points, and Noah Clowney led their bench with 17 points.

The game started with the Bucks on the attack, forcing three personal fouls in the first 90 seconds and exploiting the mismatches created when four forwards and one guard are the opposing starting five. Myles Turner wasted no time pulling out his three-ball from Toronto as the Bucks quickly built a 9-2 lead. But of course, the Nets started finding their shot as the Bucks let their feet off the gas, going on a 16-2 run as Milwaukee's offence dried up like the Sahara Desert. The Bucks didn't score another field goal until the 4:08 mark, when Jaime Jaquez Jr. finally buried a three, which set off an 8-2 run that included a sweet baseline dunk by Kel'el Ware. Despite being in the bonus for more than eight minutes, the Bucks couldn't take advantage, shooting only six free throws for the quarter and ending the period down 28-24.

The second quarter started where the first quarter ended: with Ryan Rollins throwing up ill-advised shots—he was bad in this one. Meanwhile, the Nets steadily built a 41-28 lead through the first five minutes. Thankfully, the young Bucks off the bench played their typical brand of high-energy defense and held Brooklyn to six points over the next five minutes, but unfortunately, the home side couldn't buy a bucket themselves. Milwaukee did at least outscore Brooklyn 11-4 in the final few minutes to cut the deficit to four, with Ware giving fans a reason to get hyped with a monster dunk before Jaquez scored a quick lay-up to end the half on a high note. Milwaukee headed into the locker room down 47-51.

Coming out of halftime, the Bucks kept up the pace and quickly regained the lead, largely thanks to 11 quick points from Herro, and it finally felt like the home team had found a flow. Unfortunately, that flow ended as Rollins decided to dominate the ball, force shots, and make head-scratching decisions in general. Eventually, the Nets crept back ahead after another scoring drought by the Bucks late in the third quarter. If not for Ware imposing his will in the paint, the Nets may have run away with it. Instead, the big man finished the third quarter with 10 points and 10 rebounds, scoring the final four to keep the deficit at six, 77-71.

The Nets extended their lead to 10 pretty quickly to open the final frame, as the Bucks struggled to get paint touches, something that seemed like it would come easier after getting in the paint so effectively against Philadelphia and Toronto. Milwaukee made a few pushes, but each one ended in an unforced error; they just couldn't get out of their own way. The game ended ugly as the Bucks turned it over nine times in the fourth quarter alone to give this one away. One of the more disappointing efforts of the last few years, frankly, and that's saying something.

26: the number of free throw attempts the Nets had, which allowed them to hang in the game despite their horrid shooting.

Ryan Rollins needs to be glued to the bench for a game (or five).

#2940. Former teammate calls Aaron Rodgers' Mike McCarthy takedown 'despicable'

Aaron Rodgers' scorched earth approach with Mike McCarthy was like the drop of a bomb. The fallout from its explosion has carried further than just the current Pittsburgh Steelers roster.

Former Green Bay Packers players from the Rodgers and McCarthy era – 2006 to 2018 – have started to weigh in on how the veteran quarterback handled his split from the Steelers. Greg Jennings, the wide receiver who spent seven years in Green Bay, and guard T.J. Lang, an eight-year Packer, both expressed their surprise at Rodgers' approach.

Neither player confirmed the quarterback's stories about McCarthy, and both had their criticisms for his explosive appearance on ESPN's "Pat McAfee Show" on Wednesday, Nov. 25.

AARON RODGERS: Veteran QB goes scorched earth on Mike McCarthy after Steelers benching

Rodgers' scathing comments about McCarthy came hours after the coach benched the quarterback in favor of Mason Rudolph late Tuesday, Nov. 24. In the aftermath of the "Pat McAfee Show" appearance, Pittsburgh waived the veteran quarterback ahead of Week 12.

Here's what Jennings and Lang said about Rodgers' comments:

The former Packers wide receiver said he could not corroborate any of Rodgers' stories about McCarthy during the time they shared in Green Bay. He also noted his surprise at the lack of questions for A.J. Hawk, a former linebacker for the Packers who spent the 2006 through 2014 seasons in Green Bay and a mainstay on the "Pat McAfee Show."

"A large part of me just wanted someone to ask A.J., who was sitting right there, 'A.J., do you recall any of this stuff?' Please, someone, ask A.J., 'Can you confirm, or can you second any of this?'" Jennings said on FS1.

The former receiver said he also reached out to some of his other previous teammates in Green Bay, who also said they could not confirm any of Rodgers' stories about McCarthy.

Lang, who spent the final two years of his career with the Lions, made an appearance on Detroit sports radio station 97.1 The Ticket on Wednesday, Nov. 25. He said Rodgers' decision to air out all of his grievances publicly was the exact kind of behavior the quarterback used to call out.

"I think it's despicable," Lang said. "I think that Aaron Rodgers just became everything that he has b----ed about and moaned about over the last 10 years with former coaches, former teammates. Airing grievances out, giving opinions out in the media.

"He has blacklisted I don't know how many former teammates from just simple comments telling the truth because he was sick of it."

The two-time Pro Bowler also cast doubt on some of Rodgers' comments, given that the Steelers chose to bench, then release the quarterback. Lang specifically called Rodgers out for saying he and Steelers general manager Omar Khan shared laughs about McCarthy's miscues as the head coach behind his back.

"Then why would Omar Khan side with Mike McCarthy? Why would Omar Khan allow Mike McCarthy to bench Aaron Rodgers and basically side with Mike McCarthy?" Lang said.
